#### 9. [Shared password manager for families?](https://lobste.rs/s/eccbci/shared_password_manager_for_families)
📅 Thu, 29 Oct 2026 14:39:51 GMT
👤 By: icefox | 📌 Source: Lobste.rs

Long story short, I need to have a bunch of shared accounts with family for finance and insurance and stuff, mostly for the sake of family members who are getting old and need help keeping things organized.  So the requirements are:

* Shared over long distances; just having each of us have our own password manager won't work well
* Usable for non-techie people, so no command line stuff
* I can self-host a web thing if necessary
* Works on Linux and MacOS/iOS

I *would* just use 1Password, but recent events have demonstrated them as not exactly competent.  I could always use KeepassXC and keep the file in a Google Drive folder, or maybe teach my siblings to use Syncthing, but that feels like extra steps we could do without.  Anyone have any recommendations for how to do this well?

🏷️ Tags: `ask`, `security`

#### 9. [Shared password manager for families?](https://lobste.rs/s/eccbci/shared_password_manager_for_families)
📅 Thu, 29 Oct 2026 14:39:51 GMT
👤 By: icefox | 📌 Source: Lobste.rs

Long story short, I need to have a bunch of shared accounts with family for finance and insurance and stuff, mostly for the sake of family members who are getting old and need help keeping things organized.  So the requirements are:

* Shared over long distances; just having each of us have our own password manager won't work well
* Usable for non-techie people, so no command line stuff
* I can self-host a web thing if necessary
* Works on Linux and MacOS/iOS

I *would* just use 1Password, but recent events have demonstrated them as not exactly competent.  I could always use KeepassXC and keep the file in a Google Drive folder, or maybe teach my siblings to use Syncthing, but that feels like extra steps we could do without.  Anyone have any recommendations for how to do this well?

🏷️ Tags: `ask`, `security`

Fourteen Apache-2.0 and MIT open-weight models that fit one TPU v5e chip, served one after another through vLLM on the same pinned image and scored on GSM8K and BFCL against Gemma 4 E2B. Gemma 4 E2B from BF16 to Q2_K, speed against GSM8K accuracy, on an AWS L4 with byte-identical GGUF files and the same llama.cpp build as the Tesla T4. #### 9. [Shared password manager for families?](https://lobste.rs/s/eccbci/shared_password_manager_for_families)
📅 Thu, 29 Oct 2026 14:39:51 GMT
👤 By: icefox | 📌 Source: Lobste.rs

Long story short, I need to have a bunch of shared accounts with family for finance and insurance and stuff, mostly for the sake of family members who are getting old and need help keeping things organized.  So the requirements are:

* Shared over long distances; just having each of us have our own password manager won't work well
* Usable for non-techie people, so no command line stuff
* I can self-host a web thing if necessary
* Works on Linux and MacOS/iOS

I *would* just use 1Password, but recent events have demonstrated them as not exactly competent.  I could always use KeepassXC and keep the file in a Google Drive folder, or maybe teach my siblings to use Syncthing, but that feels like extra steps we could do without.  Anyone have any recommendations for how to do this well?

🏷️ Tags: `ask`, `security`

#### 9. [Shared password manager for families?](https://lobste.rs/s/eccbci/shared_password_manager_for_families)
📅 Thu, 29 Oct 2026 14:39:51 GMT
👤 By: icefox | 📌 Source: Lobste.rs

Long story short, I need to have a bunch of shared accounts with family for finance and insurance and stuff, mostly for the sake of family members who are getting old and need help keeping things organized.  So the requirements are:

* Shared over long distances; just having each of us have our own password manager won't work well
* Usable for non-techie people, so no command line stuff
* I can self-host a web thing if necessary
* Works on Linux and MacOS/iOS

I *would* just use 1Password, but recent events have demonstrated them as not exactly competent.  I could always use KeepassXC and keep the file in a Google Drive folder, or maybe teach my siblings to use Syncthing, but that feels like extra steps we could do without.  Anyone have any recommendations for how to do this well?

🏷️ Tags: `ask`, `security`

#### 1. [Performance vs Quality: Gemma 4 E2B Quantization on an NVIDIA L4 and a Tesla T4](https://dev.to/gde/performance-vs-quality-gemma-4-e2b-quantization-on-an-nvidia-l4-and-a-tesla-t4-4b90)
📅 Thu, 29 Oct 2026 14:39:09 GMT
👤 By: xbill | 📌 Source: Dev.to

![Performance vs Quality: Gemma 4 E2B Quantization on an NVIDIA L4 and a Tesla T4](https://media2.dev.to/dynamic/image/width=1000,height=420,fit=cover,gravity=auto,format=auto/https%3A%2F%2Fraw.githubusercontent.com%2Fxbill9%2Fgemma-graveyard%2Fmain%2Fdocs%2Fdevto-cover-l4.82c50f0e.jpg)

Gemma 4 E2B from BF16 to Q2_K, speed against GSM8K accuracy, on an AWS L4 with byte-identical GGUF files and the same llama.cpp build as the Tesla T4. The L4 decodes 1.25 to 1.89 times faster, Q5_K_M is still the best trade, and switching GPUs changes nearly as many grades as quantizing from BF16 to Q5_K_M.

#### 10. [Shared password manager for families?](https://lobste.rs/s/eccbci/shared_password_manager_for_families)
📅 Thu, 29 Oct 2026 14:39:51 GMT
👤 By: icefox | 📌 Source: Lobste.rs

Long story short, I need to have a bunch of shared accounts with family for finance and insurance and stuff, mostly for the sake of family members who are getting old and need help keeping things organized.  So the requirements are:

* Shared over long distances; just having each of us have our own password manager won't work well
* Usable for non-techie people, so no command line stuff
* I can self-host a web thing if necessary
* Works on Linux and MacOS/iOS

I *would* just use 1Password, but recent events have demonstrated them as not exactly competent.  I could always use KeepassXC and keep the file in a Google Drive folder, or maybe teach my siblings to use Syncthing, but that feels like extra steps we could do without.  Anyone have any recommendations for how to do this well?

🏷️ Tags: `ask`, `security`

Fourteen Apache-2.0 and MIT open-weight models that fit one TPU v5e chip, served one after another through vLLM on the same pinned image and scored on GSM8K and BFCL against Gemma 4 E2B. Qwen3-4B matches Gemma's accuracy at 0.57x its output, SmolLM3-3B and Granite 3.3 2B match it on tool calls, and Gemma keeps the lead on long prompts.

Fourteen Apache-2.0 and MIT open-weight models that fit one TPU v5e chip, served one after another through vLLM on the same pinned image and scored on GSM8K and BFCL against Gemma 4 E2B. Qwen3-4B matches Gemma's accuracy at 0.57x its output, SmolLM3-3B and Granite 3.3 2B match it on tool calls, and Gemma keeps the lead on long prompts.
